# Decision ledger

This is the append-only recovery ledger for AHepi/miniReason. Each dated receipt records the choice, its reason, its contribution to the project goal, and evidence or completion state. These are concise decision justifications, not private model deliberations. Append corrections and outcomes; do not rewrite earlier receipts. A future agent reads README, PURPOSE, STATUS and this ledger before work. Every new design, implementation, experiment, interpretation, delegation, publication or stopping choice needs a receipt. Mechanical executions of an already recorded choice belong to that receipt’s evidence.

The end goal is to discover how model-proposed languages and semantic classes expand or hinder expression, construction, criticism and use through Mini, compared with bare, matched-call and native-reasoning controls. Prose remains legitimate. Operational completion never certifies creativity.

### D012 — Select a bounded five-configuration reason-use comparison

Choice: freeze original, independently reviewed recoded, different-reason and omitted supplemental criticisms as E016–E019, each with bare/native/matched/matched-native/Mini/Mini-native controls; freeze one shared no-return baseline E020 with only the four two-stage controls. Run configurations in this declared order, publishing each completed record before the next. Why: these contrasts separate supplemental content, expression and account availability; repeating identical no-return use requests for every reason condition would add samples rather than distinct interventions. Contribution: examines a concrete mechanism by which criticism and language affect subsequent use. One repetition witnesses finite cases only and does not isolate sampling variance or general creativity. Keep the inherited provider, explicit thinking settings and 32,768 completion ceiling, record actual usage, and use at most five concurrent arm calls within one test. No random ordering, padding or optimization is introduced. If provider access fails, preserve that configuration, publish its failure and stop successor dispatch until the external condition changes. Preparation may continue offline.

### D021 — Check what the route comparison can identify

Choice: commission a separate source/plan-based analysis of direct-versus-Mini conditional request equivalence and the limits of causal attribution in this block. Why: matching inputs controls confounding but can also make the two routes observationally equivalent under the same provider behavior; different sampled replies alone cannot then demonstrate a harness benefit. Contribution: explains which part of the language/criticism experiment is informative and which claimed Mini advantage the instrument cannot establish. The analysis must separate exact request equivalence, assumptions about provider state/distributions, observed outcomes and persistence/recovery effects. It may use current source and frozen plans without anticipating E019/E020 results; no code/input changes or new provider calls are authorized by this review. It will inform the final phase disposition and remaining exact research task.

### R20260912-04 — Build a finite explicit-argument diagnostic outside frozen engine source

Timestamp: 2026-09-12T03:55:58.304436+00:00. Choice: delegate a small standard-library propositional checker and deterministic feedback renderer, with operator-authored fixtures, outside src so E023/E024 remain source-bound unchanged. Contract: finite named Boolean atoms and structured formulas (not arbitrary executable text); exhaustive valuation under a declared small atom/formula limit. Check satisfiability of premises before entailment; classify satisfiable premises as entailed, refuted (negation entailed), or undetermined using both countermodel directions; inconsistent premises never certify a claim. Malformed or out-of-scope input gives a diagnostic and leaves prose legitimate. Preserve original question, atom meanings, assumptions, goal and opaque prose objections; no automatically accepted repair, numeric creativity score, or semantic truth verdict.

Why: the user proposes checking explicit formal reasoning to inform next action, and checker-specific gains/losses are testable without the blocked provider. Contribution: isolate checker function and formalization failure mechanisms while leaving later model benefit an open empirical question. Expected operator-authored controls: outright contradiction, conditional entailment, false implication, underdetermination, consistent inadequate translation, meaning-collision/recoding examples, and refusal beyond supported scope. The formal checker cannot adjudicate mapping adequacy; those contrasts require a separate case argument. A transparent suggestion can request premise/translation review, request a counterexample response, or distinguish two compatible models, but never choose epistemic endorsement. Root will review actual outputs and publish code, fixtures and evidence before a separate report. New prototype agent owns staging files only and atomic activity appends.

REC-20260914-E opened at 2026-09-14 00:29:29 UTC: Add .github/workflows/tests.yml as an automatic offline gate on every push and pull request, and add work/ to .gitignore. Choice: a new workflow, with .github/workflows/e028-recovery.yml left byte-for-byte unchanged because its live path spends provider money and must stay workflow_dispatch only. Why: the recovery workflow is the repository's only workflow; it never installs the package and runs four focused modules, roughly 45 of the 681 offline tests, so nothing currently checks the instrument automatically. Design: triggers push (all branches) and pull_request; ubuntu-24.04; matrix over Python 3.11 and 3.12 with fail-fast disabled so one interpreter's result does not hide the other's; actions/checkout and actions/setup-python pinned to the same commit SHAs the recovery workflow already uses, 11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 and a26af69be951a213d495a4c3e4e4022e16d87065, with persist-credentials false; `python -m pip install -e .` exactly as README documents, then `python -X utf8 -m unittest discover -s tests -v` and `python -X utf8 scripts/test_lean_guard.py`. Permissions follow the existing workflow's approach: `permissions: {}` at workflow level and `contents: read` on the one job, which checkout needs. No secret is referenced, no DEEPSEEK or OLLAMA variable is set, and no step can reach a provider. Installing the package is load-bearing, not cosmetic: in a checkout without it, `python -m unittest discover -s tests` finds 261 tests and 24 ModuleNotFoundError imports because discovery's top-level directory hides the package, while an importable package yields the full 681. Verified before publication: the E028 module runs all 14 of its tests in a fresh depth-1 clone, so the default shallow checkout does not break the test that re-clones the repository. Honest consequence: this gate will report failure on every run until the stages_entered defect in REC-20260914-D is resolved through a successor plan, because the suite really does have one failing test. That is the intended behavior of a gate; the workflow is not weakened to hide it. work/ is ignored because the recovery workflow writes work/e028-workflow and work/e028-preflight into the checkout, and those artifacts must never be committed. Published to working branch by session mandate; main publication pending owner merge. Previous verified publication: To https://github.com/AHepi/miniReason

REC-20260914-H opened at 2026-09-14 00:47:12 UTC: Correct the E028 checkpointed test's final assertion and normalize the one unpinned stage-sequence writer. This is an assertion correction plus an unpinned normalization; it is not a fix of the frozen adapter, and it does not reopen the stop recorded in REC-20260914-D or supersede OPS-20260914-STAGES. Choice: in tests/test_e028_checkpointed.py, replace the single `self.assertEqual(json.loads(remote_summary), summary)` at line 290 with a comparison of `json.loads(remote_summary)` against `json.loads(json.dumps(summary))`, carrying a one-line comment citing OPS-20260914-STAGES; and in src/minireason/campaign.py:151 write `"stages_entered": list(outcome.stages_entered)` into the `{**asdict(outcome)...}` literal, exactly as language_mini.py:365, inquiry_mini.py:354, reason_use_mini.py:386 and successor_mini.py:351 already do. Why the assertion is the right site: the property this test was written to certify is that the published remote summary bytes equal the returned summary as serialized -- it fetches summary.json out of the remote git ref and parses it -- and a Python tuple and the JSON array it serializes to are the same published bytes. Comparing a JSON-parsed object against an unserialized in-memory object compares container types that the publication boundary does not preserve, so the assertion as written tested something stronger and different from its own subject. Normalizing both sides through json.dumps/json.loads states the intended property exactly and weakens nothing else: every other assertion in the test, including status, provider_calls, reused_prefix_responses, the remote tree equality and the 13-commit count, is untouched. What this does not do: src/minireason/sql_use_return_study.py lines 324 and 416 still build the in-memory outcome with a tuple, and src/minireason/sql_construction_study.py:156 still does too. Those three sites remain defective and remain unrepairable here, because E028's plan pins 63 runtime_files including sql_use_return_study.py at 9701f7a7fef09b089643f5b7989913c8efae09a48a7453b55b54579a57ccb702 and E026 pins 60 including sql_construction_study.py, all still matching the tree, and the plan identity 0e7ada3e11b348b7c3ea46805910ece3e14fdb700975e6a22b58b44c82569b5f that the test itself asserts is a digest over those bytes. The repair of the adapters still requires a separately declared successor plan. Preconditions checked before choosing the campaign.py edit, and they confirm REC-20260914-D's finding rather than overturning it: of the 30 plan.json files under experiments/plans/ and experiments/records/, none pins src/minireason/campaign.py in runtime_files, and E028's and E026's maps in particular do not; no path under tests/ appears in any runtime_files map, so no test file is hash-pinned by any plan; campaign.py is named in 81 older-generation source maps across 67 frozen files, and its pinned hash 545659d43866d5f3b141b73e33fa875018b8b0371a5bab59a91c03e839b2d4e4 does still equal the current file, but those maps are compared whole -- reason_use_study.py:366 and successor_study.py:259 test `frozen["repository"]["files"] != current["repository"]["files"]` and the paired source_sha256 -- and every one of them is a strict subset of the live campaign.source_identity() result, short by four to eight later src/minireason modules, so each already raises its SOURCE_CHANGED before campaign.py is reached. No verification that currently passes depends on campaign.py's bytes. The JSON output of that site is unchanged in any case, because json serializes a tuple and a list identically; only the in-memory container type changes, which is what makes the four sibling adapters consistent. Because the recovered file is byte-identical to its upstream source, the missing row is a stale range citation written upstream and not a loss in extraction or in this recovery; the corrective action is the statement in `docs/mini/README.md` and erratum `SRC-002`, and the frozen engine file is deliberately not edited, since its bytes are pinned by plan `runtime_files` maps and the citation already names the right document. A fidelity check of the engine this register describes found twenty-seven of the twenty-nine files under `src/creib/forge/mini/` byte-identical between source and destination, and the two that differ, `common.py` and `openkernels.py`, already declared in the `modified` list of `docs/sources/extraction-provenance.json` and explained in its `extraction_changes`; across `src/creib/` exactly four files were mechanically edited at extraction and all four are declared. Substantive finding, and this part is interpretation rather than measurement: the string `v1.3` and the title of the harness specification occur nowhere in the fifteen recovered documents, Mini's authority chain `REQUEST.md` → `DESIGN.md` → `SPEC.md` is closed and points nowhere outside itself, and `DESIGN.md` §13 states "No status, no standing, no elimination. The prototype produces artifacts and measures; nothing is accepted, refuted, or ranked," with `SPEC.md` stating "Nothing decides anything is true, or better." Mini's lack of v1.3 status machinery is therefore a design decision recorded in Mini's own authority, not erosion of a capability it once had or claimed; that settles the provenance of the absence and does not establish that declining status machinery is right for the present research. THE PRE-REGISTRATION, stated here and not only in the register. Question: do the mechanism-level facts the published instruments report -- the facts `minireason.graph_import_h005` computes and the use-relation instrument reads -- vary across model families under identical material, identical topology and identical rendering? The facts are exactly six and only six: FCL-1 parse rate on the `mini_fcl` arm (`read_fcl1` against `parse_failure`/`schema_failure`); reference resolution (`report.resolution`: `refs`, `resolved`, `extensions`, `dangling`, `task`); cross-document `depends` usage (residue code `depends_cross_document`); the OPAQUE rate per `envelope_status`; nu-attacks (`validity_node_minted_unasserted`, `criticism_of_criticism_retargeted`, nu nodes in `att`); and reinstatement in the grounded labels. Design: 19 arms over six occurrences, one family each, all through the OpenAI-compatible path and never the `.native` Ollama path -- occurrence-01 `deepseek-flash` with `bare`, `native`, `mini_fcl`, `mini_prose`; occurrences 02-06 `ollama/gpt-oss-120b`, `ollama/qwen3.5-397b`, `ollama/glm-5.3`, `ollama/kimi-k3`, `ollama/gemma4-31b` with `bare`, `mini_fcl`, `mini_prose`. Scope is frozen in each `arms.json` as `{"problems": ["daily"], "cycles": [1]}`, pinned by `arms_sha256`, enforced as `SCOPE_EXCLUDED` in `ready_coordinates` and therefore in `prepare-wave` and `send-wave`, and re-read by `audit` as `out_of_scope`: 12 multi-call arms x 5 nodes + 6 bare arms x 1 node + 1 native arm x 1 node = **67 unique provider calls**, `max_calls` 12 on occurrence-01 and 11 on each of 02-06, against `max_calls_envelope` 168 and 156. `max_calls` is the authorisation, not a guideline. Ceilings: `max_tokens` 8192 per arm and `timeout_seconds` 180 from the endpoint record, both surfaced per arm in `plan['ceilings']`; `seed: 7` on every Ollama arm and null on DeepSeek, an asymmetry that is a property of the providers and is reported rather than corrected. **No retries at any layer**: one `complete` is one request on the wire or none, an attempted coordinate with no terminal evidence is audited and never re-sent, and one `FAILED` node ends that arm for the rest of the occurrence -- the next `prepare-wave` returns fewer coordinates and refuses nothing, so the audit signal (`counts.FAILED` >= 1 with `counts.unvisited` > 0 and that arm's `invocations` row reading `"complete": false`) is read after every round. **Five concurrent requests per credential**, the owner's authorisation, enforced in three places and tested in each: per-`key_env` admission in `ready_coordinates`; `WAVE_SIZE_INVALID` re-refusal in `send-wave` before any attempt marker or provider object exists; and a module-level gate keyed by `key_env`, acquired before the attempt marker is written, so an attempt exists only for a call this process is about to make. `provider_openai_compat.slots_for` is a fourth ceiling around the socket. Because the gate is process-wide, **one process drives all six occurrences** -- the five Ollama occurrences sharing one gate of five on `OLLAMA_API_KEY` while occurrence-01 overlaps them on `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`; two coordinator processes on one credential are not authorised, because nothing in this repository holds a ceiling across processes. What counts as evidence: only those six mechanism facts as computed by the published importer over each occurrence, plus the runner's own audit counts. Nothing else. No merit claim, no ranking, no "which family reasons better"; parser success, more objections, longer documents, more refs, more nu nodes and a larger graph are not repair; a refuted label is bookkeeping over the attack relation the authors themselves declared and invariant I7 says no label is a semantic attribution; an `accepted` label on an unattacked artifact is accept-by-position, and on a prose arm no warrant, no attack edge and no refuted label can arise at all; transport status is never a verdict. **Root alone reads the content**, and no count here may be offered as a substitute for reading the artifacts. Falsifier: the question is answered no if, across the six occurrences, every `mini_fcl` arm's FCL-1 parse rate is the same (all 5/5 or all 0/5), and `resolution.resolved / resolution.refs` and `dangling` do not differ, and `depends_cross_document`, `criticism_of_criticism_retargeted` and the nu count are equal, and the grounded label multiset over the five `mini_fcl` node artifacts is the same in every occurrence including whether any reinstatement occurs. Any one of those differing is the positive result, and the positive result is "this carrier is read differently by different families", never "family X is better". Claim ceiling: the most this study can support is that under identical frozen material and an identical rendered brief the mechanism-level facts the published instruments report did or did not vary across these six families, on this one fork5 invocation of the `daily` problem, at these ceilings, on this date. It cannot support a claim about model capability, about FCL-1's value, about Mini's scheduler (which does not execute here), about any family's behaviour on another problem or another cycle, or about any family not listed. Six families with one invocation each is six observations, not a sample.

THREE ASYMMETRIES DECLARED IN ADVANCE, each of which changes how a number may be read. First, **the OPAQUE rate of this study is not comparable with H005 occurrence-01**. The fork declares two decode repairs and no others, applied before the same strict shape check: strip one outer Markdown fence when the whole trimmed content is a single fenced block, then re-parse with `strict=False` so a raw control character inside a string does not fail the parse. Nothing is reconstructed, no field is invented, the raw returned text is stored byte-for-byte and hashed into `public_text_sha256`, and an envelope no declared repair rescues is still OPAQUE. Each repair is declared because a record shows the loss it prevents, and both records are named in the register and are transport observations rather than evidence in the study: `fence_stripped` from the provider module's staging smoke, where Ollama cloud accepted `response_format: json_object` without enforcing it and ten of thirty contentful responses came back fenced, `kimi-k3` and `gemma4:31b` among them and both in scope; `lenient_control_chars` from the owner's own frozen H005 occurrence-01 `matched` arm, `response.txt` (7,732 bytes, `054b1c43c50e4d2a581023dc73cd08439665cf18b7663681559a8ab19345df3a`) and `carry.txt` (6,505 bytes, `c61e45da74b50542ae5d07b2e4af9a18e0696c789981ca26b42c944ec3913c8e`), both of which strict `json.loads` refuses and `strict=False` parses into exactly `{body, commitments}`, both recorded COMPLETE with `envelope_status: OPAQUE` and the empty-string `commitments_sha256`. That second record is DeepSeek's, not an Ollama family's, and no Ollama record in reach shows a control-character case; the repair is declared for every family because the loss it prevents is a transport fault, and `audit.counts.lenient_control_chars` is where the run will say whether any family in scope commits it. **`strict_parse_would_succeed` is the figure comparable with H005 occurrence-01; the OPAQUE rate is not.** Both are recorded per node, and a fence is a formatting habit, not a reasoning failure. Second, **`bare` is not the same control in every occurrence**: a reasoning-disabled direct baseline on occurrence-01, where the DeepSeek arm carries `thinking: {"type": "disabled"}` on the wire, and a default direct baseline on occurrences 02-06, where those families emit reasoning by default and it cannot be switched off through this surface -- exactly the case `docs/EXPERIMENT_METHOD.md` covers -- so any cross-occurrence reading of a `bare` PARTIAL rate or token count is comparing a reasoning-off arm with reasoning-on arms. Third, **`native` is declared and run on one family only**. AGENTS.md requires the comparison bare / native reasoning / Mini, and `docs/EXPERIMENT_METHOD.md` requires that where native mode is unsupported the comparison be marked unavailable rather than dropped. DeepSeek's wire carries `thinking` plus `reasoning_effort`, which `provider_openai_compat._build_payload` emits for that family and no other, so the arm is declared and run there; on the five Ollama families it is **marked unavailable** with the runner's own refusal code `ARM_NATIVE_WIRE_UNKNOWN`, raised at plan time before any call is spent, and `Endpoint.native` is a different axis (Ollama's own `/api/chat`) and not a thinking mode. The native comparison is therefore available on one of six families and is run there; it is not silently discharged for DeepSeek by a limitation belonging to the other five. A fourth consequence is recorded rather than enforced: reasoning tokens count against `max_tokens` on every Ollama family in scope except `gemma4:31b`, so a family may reach `finish_reason: length` and be recorded PARTIAL where DeepSeek would not -- a property of the transport budget, not of the contribution -- and `reasoning_content_present` is recorded per node rather than failing it, hidden reasoning being a custody failure only where the runner set a thinking control and set it to disabled. `reasoning_content_persisted` remains a hard refusal everywhere: no native reasoning text is ever stored, returned or fed to another call.

PUBLICATION BEFORE DISPATCH is the mechanism, not a convention. `send-wave` and `send-round` refuse unless HEAD equals the configured publication ref and every path in the wave's transitive closure -- plan, material, arms, manifests, the runner itself, the runtime pins, the provider module, the endpoint registry, and every request, trace, attempt, receipt, artifact, raw response text and provider call record a dependency reaches -- is byte-identical to the committed bytes. A ref that has moved is `PUBLISH_REF_CHANGED`; a file differing by one byte is `INPUT_NOT_PUBLISHED`; neither constructs a provider. That closure includes the previous round's receipts, so a round cannot be sent until the previous round's results are committed and pushed: four rounds, one push per round before dispatch plus one final push of round 4's records, five in all. This receipt is therefore the publication that makes dispatch possible, and the dispatch runs under it: the six `initialize`/`verify` freezes and their push, then four rounds of prepare-wave / push / send-round / push, then `audit` per occurrence, then the published importer `tools/import_h005.py` and the published `tools/use_relation_h005.py` over each of the six occurrences at full scope into `experiments/analyses/F001-fork5-multifamily-2026-09-14/occurrence-0N/{import,use-table}/` with a cross-occurrence README stating mechanism facts as counts only, carrying the I7 banner and the sentence that no cross-family merit reading is made there. Each round's outcome is appended to this receipt as evidence, in a short paragraph carrying that round's counts, never backdated. If a wave fails on a provider error the arm ends -- no retry, by design -- and the failure is recorded with its `failure_code` and the run continues.

The offline suite before this work is 977 tests OK with one skip; with the fork's 69 it is expected at 1046, and the outcome receipt reports the real number from `PYTHONPATH=src python -X utf8 -m unittest discover -s tests`. REC-20260914-T opened at 2026-09-14 07:05 UTC: Publish a SUCCESSOR runner for the F001 fork and run two further occurrences of the same frozen material at a raised completion ceiling. F001's occurrences 04 (`ollama/glm-5.3`) and 05 (`ollama/kimi-k3`) were the two the ceiling truncated: their published audits carry every one of the seven non-COMPLETE terminal records of the whole six-occurrence run, all with `failure_code: INCOMPLETE_GENERATION`, `finish_reason: length` and exactly 8,192 completion tokens, and **five of the seven returned zero bytes of public content** -- the whole ceiling spent on reasoning, nothing to record as a partial contribution, the arm ended. Those records stand; nothing is re-sent, relabelled, repaired or superseded. What this receipt publishes is a second ceiling beside the first. **The published runner `tools/multicycle_commitment_study_multi.py` is NOT edited**, and this is the reason it cannot be: it writes its own sha256 into every plan it has already built, as `runner_sha256` and `helper_sha256`, both folded into `plan_id`, so one changed byte would invalidate the six published plans and every custody check that reads one. The successor is a new file with its own identity, `tools/multicycle_commitment_study_multi_v2.py`: a byte copy of v1 at sha256 `a56fed415bd72395470f40c18d9985ec6fcde8a851662006a9b6fecbfe57ec80` with four differences, each marked `# V2:` in the source and listed in the file's own header -- (a) the provenance header; (b) `CAP = 8192` replaced, the bound `validate_arms` enforces becoming `MAX_CEILING = 393216`, the provider's own bound mirrored from `provider_openai_compat._RecordedCaller._validate_call_args`; (c) `manifest_for` deriving `cycles.completion_tokens_per_call` and `cycles.max_completion_tokens` from the ceilings the occurrence's arms actually declare instead of from the constant; (d) a docstring that says v2. **One declared deviation from the ruling's wording, and it is deliberate**: v1's single constant did two jobs -- the bound AND the default an arm that declares no `max_tokens` receives -- so replacing it outright would have moved the default from 8,192 to 393,216 as well, a behavioural change the ceiling question does not need. v2 therefore splits the constant by role, `MAX_CEILING = 393216` for the bound and `DEFAULT_CEILING = 8192` for the default, so that an undeclared arm is settled exactly as v1 settles it and the raised bound really is the only behavioural difference between the runners; the alternative would have made the ruling's own requirement -- the v1 suite re-pointed with only the `ARM_CEILING` case changed -- impossible, since a dozen v1 assertions read 8,192 off the default. The claim is proved, not asserted: `tests/test_multicycle_commitment_study_multi_v2.py::V2DiffProofTests` normalises the module docstring away, diffs v2 against v1 and asserts the only hunks are the ten declared, that each is marked `# V2:`, that the only definitions differing from v1 are the six the ceiling touches, and that where no arm declares a ceiling v2's arm mapping and manifest bytes are v1's to the byte. Pre-registration is appended to `experiments/diagnostics/F001-fork5-multifamily/PLAN.md` (append-only, dated section "Successor occurrences 07 and 08 under runner v2") **before** anything is initialized: same material, same arms, same scope `{"problems": ["daily"], "cycles": [1]}`, same seed 7, endpoints `ollama/glm-5.3` (occurrence-07) and `ollama/kimi-k3` (occurrence-08), per-arm `max_tokens` 32768, **22 provider calls**, `automatic_retries` 0, reasoning not manipulated in any way -- no thinking control is set, requested or disabled on either family and none can be through this surface -- and a cell that hits the raised ceiling stays PARTIAL or unresolved, preserved and never relabelled. 32,768 is not predicted to be sufficient and nothing here treats it as a fix. Identity disclosure, measured before this work: `runtime_files` over 60 paths digests to `4365421b30be43408ec96c69f21d30db6996de5bff6b6adeba7a138b6252f356` and `campaign.source_identity()` over 108 files digests to `e198b1a5fdceb946ba95ed8655d4d6658861f138c29328a2cdf6e8cacf3b3b19`; both are **expected to be unchanged afterwards**, because every file published under this receipt is under `tools/`, `tests/`, `docs/` or `experiments/` and neither digest covers those, and the outcome receipt re-measures both and discloses what it finds rather than restating these values as still current. The offline suite before this work is **Ran 1046 tests, OK (skipped=1)**; with v2's 76 it is expected at 1122, and the v1 suite must stay at 69 OK untouched. Why: F001 planned 89 coordinates across eight occurrences and 74 are COMPLETE; the residue of 15 is exactly three classes, each read off the published records. **Ceiling-caused, 7** — `failure_code: INCOMPLETE_GENERATION` with `finish_reason: "length"` at `usage.completion_tokens` exactly 8192, the arm's whole declared `max_tokens`, every one of them carrying `reasoning_content_present: true`, five of the seven returning zero bytes of `content` and therefore FAILED with a null `envelope_status` and two returning content and therefore PARTIAL: occurrence-04 `ollama/glm-5.3` `mini_fcl/objection`, `mini_fcl/rival`, `mini_prose/objection`, `mini_prose/response`, and occurrence-05 `ollama/kimi-k3` `mini_fcl/rival`, `mini_fcl/response`, `mini_prose/carry`. **Timeout-caused, 2** — `failure_code: TRANSPORT_OR_RESPONSE_ERROR`, `"The read operation timed out"` at 180,368 ms and 180,456 ms against `settings.timeout_seconds: 180`, with no `finish_reason`, no usage and no content, on occurrences 07 and 08 which had ALREADY raised `max_tokens` to 32,768 under runner v2: occurrence-07 `mini_fcl/objection` and occurrence-08 `mini_fcl/carry`. **Blocked, 6** — coordinates with no `attempts` marker, no request, no provider record and no receipt, removed from the queue by the no-retry truncation rule after an upstream node of the same arm failed. Occurrences 01, 02, 03 and 06 (`deepseek-flash`, `ollama/gpt-oss-120b`, `ollama/qwen3.5-397b`, `ollama/gemma4-31b`) have **no** ceiling-caused outcome at all; the whole residue is two families. So: at 8,192 the binding constraint on those two families was the ceiling, and REC-20260914-T's raise to 32,768 answered it — but at 32,768 the binding constraint is the fixed 180-second wall clock, which REC-20260914-T itself names as an open follow-up in those words. What is dispatched: **10 calls and no more**, `max_calls` being the authorisation — two occurrences, `ollama/glm-5.3` and `ollama/kimi-k3`, one arm each (`mini_fcl`, surface `fcl`, kind `mini`, seed 7), five fork5 nodes each, scope `{"problems": ["daily"], "cycles": [1]}`, in **four rounds of 2 / 4 / 2 / 2**, both single-key on `OLLAMA_API_KEY` through one process-wide gate of five. THE WHOLE ARM AND NOT THE FOUR UNRESOLVED NODES, because fork5 is a dependency graph: `objection` and `rival` read `account`'s artifact and `response` reads `objection`'s, so a node cannot be re-asked without its inputs, and importing F001's artifacts into a differently-identified occurrence would put an artifact produced under one frozen plan inside another. `bare` and `mini_prose` are NOT declared: every one of their cells is resolved at 32,768 on occurrences 07 and 08, and the alternative of declaring all three arms — 22 calls — was considered and not taken. One cost of that choice is named rather than hidden: occurrence-07's `mini_prose/response` completed in 175,006 ms, within five seconds of the clock it ran under, so a prose control at 600 s would not have been redundant and is absent from F002 rather than reported null. Ceilings: `max_tokens` **32,768**, the figure occurrences 07 and 08 already ran at, so F002 changes one thing and not two against them; it is **not predicted to be sufficient** and nothing here treats it as a fix. Timeouts: **600 s**, up from the endpoint record's 180, which is the transport's OWN validation maximum (`minireason.provider_openai_compat.Endpoint.__post_init__` admits 1..600 and refuses 601), a bound this study reaches rather than one it invents, applied to the resolved `Endpoint` **value** by `dataclasses.replace` at dispatch so that `src/minireason/data/endpoints.json` is NEVER written. The raise is arithmetic over published records rather than a guess: F001's own 18 timed calls at 32,768 on these two families ran at **77.9 to 151.0 completion tokens per second** (mean 99.0), so a call that spends the whole 32,768 takes **217 s at the fastest and 421 s at the slowest** observed rate — inside 600 and outside 180 — while at the slower rates the same families showed at 8,192 (60.3 to 71.5 tok/s on `kimi-k3`) a full-ceiling call would take up to 543 s, inside 600 but not by much. **600 s is not predicted to be sufficient either**: a call that reaches it is a `TRANSPORT_OR_RESPONSE_ERROR` refusal recorded with its code, ending that arm, reported exactly as any other outcome. Concurrency: at most five concurrent requests per credential, held by the runner's per-key wave admission and by the module-level process-wide gate, **zero automatic retries at every layer**, write-once records with `NO_REPLAY`, and one FAILED node still ends that arm for the rest of the occurrence. A SUCCESSOR RUNNER IDENTITY IS REQUIRED AND IS NOT A PREFERENCE: `plan_body` writes the runner's own sha256 into every plan as `runner_sha256` and `helper_sha256`, folded into `plan_id`, so one changed byte of `tools/multicycle_commitment_study_multi_v2.py` would invalidate the eight F001 plan ids published under v1 and v2 and every later `verify` and `audit` that re-derives them — exactly the reason REC-20260914-T forked v1 instead of editing it, and REC-20260914-V forked the C001 driver.
